    84	 * Why: deliberate addition, not preservation — `RoutingContext` and `LifecycleContext` both widen
    85	 * `RunContext`, so this is the folder's first narrowing context type. Its proof is the call site:
    86	 * `engine.ts` hands `preflightDiffs` the whole run context and it typechecks.
    87	 */
    88	export interface DiffContext {
    89	  repoDir: string;
    90	  config: ProjectConfig;
    91	  vars: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>;
    92	  ticket: TicketRecord;
    93	  runId: number;
    94	  baseOverride: string | null;
    95	  deferredDiffs: ReadonlyMap<string, DeferredDiff>;
    96	  persistence: Pick<RunPersistence, 'appendLog'>;
    97	}
    98	
    99	/** What {@link preflightDiffs} reads: {@link DiffContext} plus the flow it walks and the maps it fills. */
   100	export interface PreflightContext extends DiffContext {
   101	  flow: Flow;
   102	  diffInputs: Map<string, string>;
   103	  deferredDiffs: Map<string, DeferredDiff>;
   104	}
   105	
   106	/**
   107	 * The one step shape both callers hand {@link materialiseDiff}: an id a failure names, and the
   108	 * `input.diff` a flow file wrote. A fan-out template site carries the synthetic `<step id>.step`.
   109	 */
   110	export interface DiffStep {
   111	  id: unknown;
   112	  input: { diff: unknown };
   113	}
   114	
   115	/** A `{…}` placeholder that survived interpolation, which only a per-task template may hold. */
   116	const TEMPLATE_HOLE = /\{[\w.]+\}/;
   117	
   118	// A range is named twice wherever it appears — interpolated, so it can be pasted into a terminal,
   119	// and as the flow file writes it, so it can be found in the file that has to change.
   120	const named = (range: string, written: string): string => `\`${range}\` (flow file: \`${written}\`)`;
   121	
   122	/** One place a flow file put an `input.diff`, and whether it is a per-task template. */
   123	interface DiffSiteEntry {
   124	  site: DiffStep;
   125	  perTask: boolean;
   126	}
   127	
   128	/** Whatever `value` is, as a bag of unknown properties — or `undefined` when it is not an object. */
   129	const loose = (value: unknown): Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> | undefined =>
   130	  typeof value === 'object' && value !== null ? value as Readonly<Record<string, unknown>> : undefined;
   131	
   132	/**
   133	 * Every place one step of a flow can carry an `input.diff`.
   134	 *
   135	 * The step itself, and — for a `fan_out` step — its `step:` template, which the fan-out copies into
   136	 * a real step and `buildPrompt` then reads like any other `input.diff`. Left out of the preflight, a
   137	 * template range escapes it twice over: a bad one fails only once the fan-out's own adapters have
   138	 * been billed, and a good one is re-materialised by every expanded task, so one range costs n git
   139	 * spawns and the members of a wave read evidence resolved at different moments. `lint/lint.ts`
   140	 * reaches the same site for the same reason, and its `flattenSteps` deliberately still does not —
   141	 * the template's id, role and adapter are placeholders the duplicate-id, goto and cross_vendor rules
   142	 * must not see. The synthetic id matches the label lint uses, so one flow file reads the same in
   143	 * both failures.
   144	 */
   145	function diffSitesOf(step: Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>): DiffSiteEntry[] {
   146	  const own = loose(step.input)?.diff;
   147	  const template = loose(loose(step.step)?.input)?.diff;
   148	  return [
   149	    ...(own ? [{ site: { id: step.id, input: { diff: own } }, perTask: false }] : []),
   150	    ...(step.fan_out && template
   151	      ? [{ site: { id: `${String(step.id)}.step`, input: { diff: template } }, perTask: true }]
   152	      : []),
   153	  ];
   154	}
   155	
   156	/** An endpoint no earlier group creates, and which therefore has no producer to name. */
   157	interface UnownedEndpoint {
   158	  side: EndpointSide;
   159	  ref: string;
   160	  class: 'template' | 'pre-existing';
   161	  step: null;
   162	}
   163	
   164	/**
   165	 * What the preflight may ask of one endpoint of an interpolated range.
   166	 *
   167	 * A union rather than one shape with a nullable `step`, so that the producer list is filtered out of
   168	 * it without a cast and a producer with no step id is unrepresentable.
   169	 */
   170	type Endpoint = EndpointProducer | UnownedEndpoint;
   171	
   172	/**
   173	 * Each endpoint of `range`, left to right, classified against the branches created before this group:
   174	 *
   175	 * - `step-created` — an earlier group of this flow creates it, so it is not due yet and the range is
   176	 *   deferred to step time. True even when the ref already exists at run start: bytes captured before
   177	 *   its producer ran are that step's PREVIOUS output.
   178	 * - `template` — a `fan_out` step's `step:` template naming a per-task variable, which has no value
   179	 *   until `tasks.yaml` is expanded. Only a template can be in this state; an outer step's unresolved
   180	 *   `{…}` is pre-existing and fails like any other ref that does not resolve.
   181	 * - `pre-existing` — everything else, including a ref only a LATER step creates. Deferring one of
   182	 *   those would move the failure past a billed step, which is what the preflight exists to prevent.
   183	 *
   184	 * Why: behaviour preserved from spike/src/engine.js — a range that is not exactly two endpoints is
   185	 * malformed and {@link materialiseDiff}'s shape guard owns that failure, so none are returned and
   186	 * the caller sends the range to that guard unchanged. Classifying the parts of a malformed range
   187	 * answers a different question.
   188	 */
   189	function classifyEndpoints(
   190	  range: string,
   191	  createdSoFar: ReadonlyMap<string, string>,
   192	  perTask: boolean,
   193	): Endpoint[] {
   194	  const refs = range.split('...');
   195	  if (refs.length !== 2) return [];
   196	  return refs.map((ref, index): Endpoint => {
   197	    const side: EndpointSide = index === 0 ? 'left' : 'right';
   198	    const step = createdSoFar.get(ref);
   199	    if (step !== undefined) return { side, ref, class: 'step-created', step };
   200	    return { side, ref, class: perTask && TEMPLATE_HOLE.test(ref) ? 'template' : 'pre-existing', step: null };
   201	  });
   202	}
   203	
   204	// What the preflight may say about the endpoint that is NOT due, when the other one fails. It is not
   205	// supposed to resolve — its producer has not run — so reporting it as one that does not resolve
   206	// either would be the same category error the diagnosis half exists to remove. Reached only for an
   207	// endpoint whose class is not `pre-existing`, since that is the class that failed.
   208	const notDueClause = (endpoint: Endpoint | undefined, site: DiffStep): string | null => {
   209	  if (endpoint === undefined) return null;
   210	  return endpoint.class === 'step-created'
   211	    ? `the ${endpoint.side} endpoint ${endpoint.ref} is not created until step "${endpoint.step}" runs`
   212	    : `the ${endpoint.side} endpoint ${endpoint.ref} is a per-task template with no value until "${String(site.id)}" expands its tasks`;
   213	};
   214	
   215	/** The evidence {@link missingEndpointFailure} words around its identifying phrase. */
   216	interface MissingEndpoint {
   217	  side: EndpointSide;
   218	  ref: string;
   219	  range: string;
   220	  written: string;
   221	  /** What is known about the endpoint beside it; `null` entries are dropped. */
   222	  clauses: ReadonlyArray<string | null>;
   223	  /** The run's effective diff anchor, passed in so one place writes that fallback down. */
   224	  base: string;
   225	}
   226	
   227	/**
   228	 * The failure for an endpoint that does not resolve, raised by the preflight and by
   229	 * {@link materialiseDiff} alike — so which layer noticed does not change what a maintainer reads.
   230	 *
   231	 * The three identifying phrases are chosen by the failing endpoint's own identity and are matched by
   232	 * substring in existing fixtures; `clauses` are the evidence added around them, and are the only
   233	 * part the two callers word differently.
   234	 */
   235	function missingEndpointFailure(step: DiffStep, context: DiffContext, detail: MissingEndpoint): FlowError {
   236	  const { side, ref, range, written, clauses, base } = detail;
   237	  const integration = integrationBranch(context.ticket.meta.id);
   238	  const tail = `${[`it is the ${side} endpoint of ${named(range, written)}`, ...clauses]
   239	    .filter((clause): clause is string => Boolean(clause)).join('; ')}. Neither the diff nor the containment check was run.`;
   240	  if (ref === base) {
   241	    // Why: preserved behaviour, see Q-0038 — keyed on whether the run was GIVEN --base, never on
   242	    // whether its value differs from repo.base_branch: an override may legitimately name the
   243	    // configured value and the maintainer still typed it. An absent field is no override, so a
   244	    // hand-built context keeps the configured wording. Supersedes the Q-0006 review-runtime contract
   245	    // for the override path only, per Q-0038 errata E-1.
   246	    return new FlowError(context.baseOverride != null
   247	      ? `--base names missing ref "${ref}" — ${tail}`
   248	      : `repo.base_branch in harness/harness.yaml names missing ref "${base}" — ${tail}`);
   249	  }
   250	  if (ref === integration) {
   251	    return new FlowError(`ticket ${context.ticket.meta.id}: expected ${integration}; review requires an integrated branch — ${tail}`);
   252	  }
   253	  return new FlowError(`${String(step.id)}: input.diff names missing ref "${ref}" — ${tail}`);
   254	}
   255	
   256	/**
   257	 * The prompt section one diff-bearing step reads: the range's `--stat` and its patch, truncated to
   258	 * the configured byte limit at a character boundary.
   259	 *
   260	 * @throws {FlowError} when the range is out of class, when an endpoint does not resolve, or when the
   261	 *   range is empty — each with the evidence that exists and no claim git did not return.
   262	 */
   263	export function materialiseDiff(step: DiffStep, context: DiffContext): string {
   264	  const written = String(step.input.diff);
   265	  const range = interpolate(written, context.vars);
   266	  const base = String(context.vars.base ?? context.config.repo?.base_branch ?? DEFAULT_BASE_BRANCH);
   267	  // The guard forbids a flow file aiming input.diff at refs unrelated to this ticket — a merge
   268	  // commit, another ticket's branch, an arbitrary SHA. It used to demand exactly
   269	  // `{base}...{integration}`, which was the review flow's shape and only that: the chore flow reviews
   270	  // integration...implement and the stale guard rejected it the day it landed. Both endpoints must be
   271	  // the configured base or one of this ticket's own branches, and the guard composes with `--base`
   272	  // because `base` is ctx.vars.base. Its static twin lives in `lint/lint.ts`. See Q-0034, Q-0077.
   273	  const ticketPrefix = ticketBranchPrefix(context.ticket.meta.id);
   274	  const [left, right, ...extra] = range.split('...');
   275	  const related = (ref: string): boolean => ref === base || ref.startsWith(ticketPrefix);
   276	  if (!left || !right || extra.length || !related(left) || !related(right)) {
   277	    throw new FlowError(`${String(step.id)}: input.diff must relate the configured base or this ticket's own branches ("${base}", "${ticketPrefix}…") with "...", got ${range}`);
   278	  }
   279	  // Which endpoint an earlier step of THIS flow was supposed to create, when the preflight deferred
   280	  // this range. Naming that step is the difference between telling the reader a branch is missing and
     1	// Q-0051: the diff preflight and materialisation, ported from spike/src/engine.js.
     2	//
     3	// Every fixture builds its own throwaway repository. Nothing here asserts the containment state of
     4	// a branch in THIS repository — that would be red only until the next landing and green forever
     5	// after, which the permanent-acceptance-test decision (2026-08-23) exists to prevent.
     6	//
     7	// Two habits carried across from q0035-empty-range.js, both from that ticket's risk list. No whole
     8	// sentence is asserted: required evidence must be present and prohibited claims absent, because a
     9	// punctuation-sensitive snapshot breaks on every rewording and proves nothing. And a short SHA is
    10	// never assumed to be a fixed width — git chooses the abbreviation.
    11	import { execFileSync } from 'node:child_process';
    12	import fs from 'node:fs';
    13	import path from 'node:path';
    14	
    15	import YAML from 'yaml';
    16	import { afterAll, afterEach, describe, expect, test, vi } from 'vitest';
    17	
    18	import type { Event, Flow } from '@quorum/shared';
    19	
    20	import { loadProject } from '../backlog/project.js';
    21	import type { TicketRecord } from '../backlog/backlog.js';
    22	import { coreSourceFiles } from '../../test/corpus.js';
    23	import { commitAll, counting, git, removeTempDirs, tempDir, write } from '../../test/repo.js';
    24	import * as diffModule from './diff.js';
    25	import { materialiseDiff, preflightDiffs, trimIncompleteUtf8Suffix } from './diff.js';
    26	import type { DeferredDiff, DiffContext, DiffStep, EndpointProducer, PreflightContext } from './diff.js';
    27	import { runFlow } from './engine.js';
    28	import { loadFlow } from './loaders.js';
    29	import * as routing from './routing.js';
    30	import { FlowError } from './types.js';
    31	import type { RunFlowOptions, RunContext } from './types.js';
    32	
    33	afterAll(removeTempDirs);
    34	afterEach(() => { vi.restoreAllMocks(); });
    35	
    36	/** Q-0035 AC-2's list: what a message may never claim, since an ancestry check establishes none of it. */
    37	const FORBIDDEN = /\b(merged|landed|shipped|rebased|cherry-picked|reset)\b|already in\b/i;
    38	
    39	const TICKET = 'T-9';
    40	const BRANCH = `harness/${TICKET}/integration`;
    41	const WRITTEN = '{base}...harness/{id}/integration';
    42	const STEP: DiffStep = { id: 'review-claude', input: { diff: WRITTEN } };
    43	
    44	/** A repository with one commit on main and, unless told otherwise, the ticket branch beside it. */
    45	function repoWith({ branch = true } = {}): string {
    46	  const root = tempDir('q0051-');
    47	  git(root, 'init', '-q', '-b', 'main');
    48	  write(path.join(root, 'a.txt'), 'one\n');
    49	  commitAll(root, 'base');
    50	  if (branch) git(root, 'branch', BRANCH);
    51	  return root;
    52	}
    53	
    54	/** A commit on `branch` carrying `tree`, made without checking anything out. */
    55	function branchAt(root: string, branch: string, tree: string, parent: string, message: string): void {
    56	  const sha = git(root, '-c', 'user.email=q@a', '-c', 'user.name=qa', 'commit-tree', tree, '-p', parent, '-m', message);
    57	  git(root, 'branch', branch, sha);
    58	}
    59	
    60	/**
    61	 * A ticket record with the fields this module reads — `meta.id` — and the rest filled in so no cast
    62	 * is needed. `appendLog` only ever passes it back to the caller's own sink.
    63	 */
    64	const ticketRecord = (dir: string): TicketRecord => ({
    65	  dir, folder: `${TICKET}-diff`, body: 'body\n',
    66	  meta: {
    67	    id: TICKET, title: 'diff', stage: 'draft', owner: 'qa', repos: [], branch: BRANCH,
    68	    priority: 'p1', created: '2026-08-30', iterations: {}, history: [],
    69	  },
    70	});
    71	
    72	/** The narrowed context {@link materialiseDiff} reads, over a throwaway repository. */
    73	function contextFor(repoDir: string, vars: Record<string, unknown>, overrides: Partial<DiffContext> = {}): DiffContext {
    74	  return {
    75	    repoDir,
    76	    config: { repo: { base_branch: 'main' } },
    77	    vars,
    78	    ticket: ticketRecord(repoDir),
    79	    runId: 1,
    80	    baseOverride: null,
    81	    deferredDiffs: new Map<string, DeferredDiff>(),
    82	    persistence: { appendLog: () => { /* no sink in a direct call */ } },
    83	    ...overrides,
    84	  };
    85	}
    86	
    87	/**
    88	 * One producer as the preflight records it — the classified endpoint itself, `class` and all, which
    89	 * is what `toStrictEqual` over a real run's map proves it stores.
    90	 */
    91	const producer = (side: 'left' | 'right', ref: string, step: string): EndpointProducer =>
    92	  ({ side, ref, step, class: 'step-created' });
    93	
    94	/** Whatever `materialiseDiff` threw, or `null` when it returned. */
    95	function failure(step: DiffStep, context: DiffContext): Error | null {
    96	  try { materialiseDiff(step, context); } catch (error) { return error as Error; }
    97	  return null;
    98	}
    99	
   100	/**
   101	 * Q-0035 AC-1's five elements, asserted together: a message missing any one of them cannot be
   102	 * re-checked by hand, which is the whole point.
   103	 */
   104	function assertEvidence(
   105	  message: string,
   106	  { root, left = 'main', right = BRANCH, range, written, outcome }:
   107	  { root: string; left?: string; right?: string; range: string; written: string; outcome: string },
   108	): void {
   109	  const leftSha = git(root, 'rev-parse', '--short', left);
   110	  const rightSha = git(root, 'rev-parse', '--short', right);
   111	  expect(message, 'names the interpolated range').toContain(`\`${range}\``);
   112	  expect(message, 'names the range as the flow file writes it').toContain(`\`${written}\``);
   113	  expect(message).toContain(left);
   114	  expect(message).toContain(right);
   115	  expect(message, `names the left short SHA ${leftSha}`).toContain(leftSha);
   116	  expect(message, `names the right short SHA ${rightSha}`).toContain(rightSha);
   117	  expect(message, 'quotes the check it ran').toContain(`git merge-base --is-ancestor ${right} ${left}`);
   118	  expect(message, `states the outcome "${outcome}"`).toContain(outcome);
   119	  expect(message, 'the message claims a historical event').not.toMatch(FORBIDDEN);
   120	}
   121	
   122	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   123	// AC-1 / AC-2 — the module's shape
   124	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   125	
   126	describe('Q-0051 AC-1/AC-2 — the module boundary', () => {
   127	  test('AC-2 — the exported contract is exactly three symbols', () => {
   128	    expect(Object.keys(diffModule).sort()).toStrictEqual(['materialiseDiff', 'preflightDiffs', 'trimIncompleteUtf8Suffix']);
   129	    for (const value of Object.values(diffModule)) expect(typeof value).toBe('function');
   130	  });
   131	
   132	  test('AC-2 — RunContext satisfies the narrowed context, and one missing deferredDiffs does not', () => {
   133	    // The compile-time half. `engine.ts` hands `preflightDiffs` the whole run context and typechecks,
   134	    // which is the positive proof; this is the negative one, and each directive fails the build if
   135	    // the line it guards ever starts compiling.
   136	    const full = contextFor(repoWith(), { base: 'main', id: TICKET });
   137	    const asRunContextField: (context: RunContext) => DiffContext = (context) => context;
   138	    // @ts-expect-error a context with no deferredDiffs cannot satisfy the narrowed type (AC-2)
   139	    const missing: DiffContext = { ...full, deferredDiffs: undefined };
   140	    expect(typeof asRunContextField).toBe('function');
   141	    expect(missing.repoDir).toBe(full.repoDir);
   142	  });
   143	
   144	  test('AC-1 — the folder\'s own guard is derived from the folder, so a seventh file cannot go unscanned', () => {
   145	    // Demonstrated rather than asserted, as AC-1's Test: line requires. `q0050.source.test.ts`'s
   146	    // AC-9d scan used a hard-coded six-name array where every other check in that file derives from
   147	    // `production`, so it would have stayed GREEN over `diff.ts` — a check blind to its own subject,
   148	    // in a guard written after the decision that named the class.
   149	    const SIX = ['channel.ts', 'engine.ts', 'lifecycle.ts', 'loaders.ts', 'routing.ts', 'types.ts'];
   150	    const engine = new Map(coreSourceFiles()
   151	      .filter(([name]) => name.startsWith('engine/'))
   152	      .map(([name, text]) => [name.slice('engine/'.length), text] as const));
   153	    const production = [...engine.keys()];
   154	    expect(production).toContain('diff.ts');
   155	    // An identity, not a floor: `SIX` is frozen as the array the old guard named, so what this
   156	    // states is exactly which files the folder has gained since and which that array cannot see.
   157	    // Q-0052 adds two more and Q-0053 two more again, and a length check would have absorbed every
   158	    // one of them silently. Entering them here is the deliberate act this identity exists to force.
   159	    expect(production.filter((name) => !SIX.includes(name)))
   160	      .toStrictEqual(['composite.ts', 'diff.ts', 'prompt.ts', 'steps.ts', 'suite-output.ts']);
   161	
   162	    // The violation the guard exists to catch, injected into the one file the old array cannot see.
   163	    const violating = (name: string): string =>
   164	      `${engine.get(name) ?? ''}${name === 'diff.ts' ? '\nfunction resetTaskBranch(): void {}\n' : ''}`;
   165	    const scan = (names: readonly string[]): boolean =>
   166	      /(?:reset|delete|remove)TaskBranch/i.test(names.map(violating).join('\n'));
   167	    expect(scan(SIX), 'the hard-coded array reports green over a violation in the seventh file').toBe(false);
   168	    expect(scan(production), 'derived from the folder, the same scan sees it').toBe(true);
   169	  });
   170	
   171	  test('AC-1 — diff.ts spells neither of the two tokens git.ts owns', () => {
   172	    // git.source.test.ts iterates EVERY core source file and asserts both appear in git/git.ts and
   173	    // nowhere else, so a verbatim comment port turns another module's landed suite red. Asserted here
   174	    // too, because a criterion of this ticket should fail in this ticket's own suite.
   175	    const source = coreSourceFiles().find(([name]) => name === 'engine/diff.ts');
   176	    expect(source, 'corpus missing: packages/core/src/engine/diff.ts').toBeDefined();
   177	    for (const needle of ['merge-base', '--is-ancestor']) expect(source![1]).not.toContain(needle);
   178	  });
   179	});

   181	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   182	// AC-3 / AC-4 — the range guard, and what --base moves
   183	// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
   184	
   185	describe('Q-0051 AC-3 — the range guard is not relaxed', () => {
   186	  test('an unrelated or malformed range fails at the guard, naming the base it was given', () => {
   187	    // q0035-empty-range.js E6's second half.
   188	    const root = repoWith();
   189	    git(root, 'branch', 'some/other-branch', 'main');
   190	    const context = contextFor(root, { base: 'main', id: TICKET });
   191	
   192	    const unrelated = failure({ id: 'review', input: { diff: 'main...some/other-branch' } }, context);
   193	    expect(unrelated?.message).toMatch(/must relate the configured base or this ticket's own branches/);
   194	    expect(unrelated?.message, 'it names the supplied range').toContain('main...some/other-branch');
   195	    expect(unrelated?.message, 'and the allowed endpoint classes').toContain(`harness/${TICKET}/`);
   196	    expect(unrelated?.message, 'a guard failure must not read as an empty-range diagnosis')
   197	      .not.toMatch(/is empty|containment|contained/);
   198	
   199	    for (const malformed of ['main..harness/T-9/integration', 'main...harness/T-9/a...harness/T-9/b', 'harness/T-9/integration', '{base}...']) {
   200	      const error = failure({ id: 'review', input: { diff: malformed } }, context);
   201	      expect(error, `the guard must refuse ${malformed}`).toBeInstanceOf(FlowError);
   202	      expect(error?.message).toMatch(/must relate the configured base or this ticket's own branches/);
   203	    }
   204	  });
   205	
   206	  test('AC-3 — a rejected range spawns no git process at all', () => {
   207	    // The refusal happens before any diff or ancestry operation, so a range aimed at an unrelated ref
   208	    // costs nothing. Counted at the process boundary rather than inferred from the message.
   209	    const root = repoWith();
   210	    git(root, 'branch', 'some/other-branch', 'main');
   211	    const context = contextFor(root, { base: 'main', id: TICKET });
   212	
   213	    const rejected = counting(() => failure({ id: 'review', input: { diff: 'main...some/other-branch' } }, context));
   214	    expect(rejected.result?.message).toMatch(/must relate the configured base/);
   215	    expect(rejected.calls, 'a rejected range must not reach git').toBe(0);
   216	
   217	    // The paired positive, which is what makes the counter discriminating: an ACCEPTED range does
   218	    // spawn git, so zero above is a property of the refusal and not of the shim.
   219	    const accepted = counting(() => failure(STEP, context));
   220	    expect(accepted.calls, 'an accepted range reaches git').toBeGreaterThan(0);
   221	  });
   222	
   223	  test('AC-3/AC-7 — the guard derives its endpoints from vars.base, so --base composes with it', () => {
   224	    // q0035-empty-range.js E7.
   225	    const root = repoWith();
   226	    git(root, 'branch', 'release');
   227	    git(root, 'checkout', '-q', BRANCH);
   228	    write(path.join(root, 'a.txt'), 'one\ntwo\n');
   229	    commitAll(root, 'work');
   230	    git(root, 'checkout', '-q', 'main');
   231	    const context = contextFor(root, { base: 'release', id: TICKET });
   232	
   233	    expect(materialiseDiff(STEP, context), 'a run given another base still materialises its diff').toMatch(/\+two/);
   234	    const error = failure({ id: 'review', input: { diff: 'release...some/other' } }, context);
   235	    expect(error?.message).toMatch(/must relate the configured base/);
   236	    expect(error?.message, 'the guard reports the base it was actually given').toContain('"release"');
   237	  });
   238	});
   239	
   240	/** One commit on main, a branch that adds a line, then main takes the branch — a CONTAINED ticket. */
   241	function containedTicket(): { root: string; beforeWork: string } {
   242	  const root = tempDir('q0051-base-');
   243	  git(root, 'init', '-q', '-b', 'main');
   244	  write(path.join(root, 'a.txt'), 'one\n');
   245	  commitAll(root, 'base');
   246	  const beforeWork = git(root, 'rev-parse', 'HEAD');
   247	  git(root, 'branch', BRANCH);
   248	  git(root, 'checkout', '-q', BRANCH);
   249	  write(path.join(root, 'a.txt'), 'one\ntwo\n');
   250	  commitAll(root, 'the ticket\'s work');
   251	  git(root, 'checkout', '-q', 'main');
   252	  git(root, '-c', 'user.email=q@a', '-c', 'user.name=qa', 'merge', '-q', '--no-ff', '-m', 'take the branch', BRANCH);
   253	  return { root, beforeWork };
   254	}
   255	
   256	describe('Q-0051 AC-4 — --base moves the diff anchor and nothing else', () => {
   257	  test('B1 — a contained ticket is unreviewable against the configured base and reviewable against the override', () => {
   258	    const { root, beforeWork } = containedTicket();
   259	    expect(failure(STEP, contextFor(root, { base: 'main', id: TICKET })),
   260	      'a contained ticket must produce an empty range against the configured base').toBeInstanceOf(FlowError);
   261	
   262	    const out = materialiseDiff(STEP, contextFor(root, { base: beforeWork, id: TICKET }));
   263	    expect(out, 'the override must yield the ticket\'s own diff').toContain('a.txt');
   264	    expect(out, 'the diff must contain the line the ticket added').toContain('two');
   265	  });
   266	
   267	  test('B2 — the override moves the anchor and leaves repo.base_branch alone', () => {
   268	    // The whole design. Implemented by overwriting the configured branch — the cheaper shape Q-0077
   269	    // refused — this reads the SHA, and a later rework or integrate step would MERGE that revision
   270	    // into the ticket's branch. This is the case that fails when materialiseDiff resolves `base` from
   271	    // configuration in preference to vars.base, and every other test here stays green.
   272	    const { root, beforeWork } = containedTicket();
   273	    const context = contextFor(root, { base: beforeWork, id: TICKET });
   274	    materialiseDiff(STEP, context);
   275	    expect(context.config.repo?.base_branch, 'repo.base_branch is what a run merges from and must not move').toBe('main');
   276	    expect(context.vars.base, 'the diff anchor is what moved').toBe(beforeWork);
   277	  });
   278	
   279	  test('B3 — the guard accepts an arbitrary revision as the base and still refuses an unrelated ref', () => {
   280	    const { root, beforeWork } = containedTicket();
   281	    materialiseDiff(STEP, contextFor(root, { base: beforeWork, id: TICKET }));
   282	    git(root, 'branch', 'harness/T-OTHER/integration');
   283	    const refused = failure(
   284	      { id: 'x', input: { diff: '{base}...harness/T-OTHER/integration' } },
   285	      contextFor(root, { base: beforeWork, id: TICKET }),
   286	    );
   287	    expect(refused, 'an endpoint belonging to another ticket must still be refused').toBeInstanceOf(FlowError);
   288	    expect(refused?.message).toMatch(/input\.diff must relate/);
   289	  });
   290	
   291	  test('B4 — with no override the anchor is the configured base, unchanged', () => {
   292	    const { root } = containedTicket();
   293	    const context = contextFor(root, { base: 'main', id: TICKET });
   294	    expect(failure(STEP, context), 'the default path is the behaviour that was there before').toBeInstanceOf(FlowError);
   295	    expect(context.vars.base).toBe('main');
   296	  });
   297	
   298	  test('B6 — an unresolvable override is blamed on the flag, and an absent field is no override', () => {
   299	    const { root } = containedTicket();
   300	    const given = contextFor(root, { base: 'no-such-revision', id: TICKET }, { baseOverride: 'no-such-revision' });
   301	    const refused = failure(STEP, given);
   302	    expect(refused, 'a revision git cannot resolve must stop the run').toBeInstanceOf(FlowError);
   303	    expect(refused?.message, 'it names the flag that supplied the value').toMatch(/--base/);
   304	    expect(refused?.message, 'it names the revision supplied').toContain('"no-such-revision"');
   305	    expect(refused?.message, 'it says which endpoint it is').toContain('left endpoint');
   306	    expect(refused?.message).toMatch(/Neither the diff nor the containment check was run/);
   307	    expect(refused?.message, 'the value did not come from configuration').not.toMatch(/repo\.base_branch/);
   308	    expect(refused?.message).not.toMatch(/harness\.yaml/);
   309	
   310	    // Attribution keys on whether the flag was TYPED, never on whether its value differs from the
   311	    // configured branch: an override may legitimately name the same value. Here the two agree.
   312	    const identical = failure(STEP, contextFor(root, { base: 'no-such-revision', id: TICKET }, {
   313	      config: { repo: { base_branch: 'no-such-revision' } }, baseOverride: 'no-such-revision',
   314	    }));
   315	    expect(identical?.message, 'an override naming the configured value is still an override').toMatch(/--base/);
   316	    expect(identical?.message).not.toMatch(/repo\.base_branch/);
   317	
   318	    // And an absent field is no override, so a hand-built context keeps the configured wording.
   319	    const configured = failure(STEP, contextFor(root, { base: 'no-such-revision', id: TICKET }));
   320	    expect(configured?.message).toMatch(/repo\.base_branch in harness\/harness\.yaml names missing ref "no-such-revision"/);
   321	    expect(configured?.message).not.toMatch(/--base/);
   322	  });
   323	});

     1	# A deferral is not a skip, and the preflight says nothing about one — 2026-09-07
     2	
     3	**Decision:** the run-level diff preflight goes on emitting **nothing** when it defers a range, and
     4	*"Q-0035 accepted: a check that skips its subject must not report success"* (2026-08-25) is **not
     5	amended**, because it does not reach this case. Q-0082 offered those two as its alternatives; the
     6	answer is neither, and no code changes.
     7	
     8	**A deferral is not a decline to examine.** The rule governs a check that could have examined its
     9	subject and did not. `docs/GLOSSARY.md`'s **Preflight** entry already states the preflight's
    10	guarantee as *per endpoint, not per range* — every endpoint that is **due** is resolved at run start,
    11	and it *"never resolves an endpoint that is not due, and never reports one as having failed to
    12	resolve."* An endpoint an earlier step of the same flow has yet to create is not a subject the
    13	preflight passed over; it is a subject that does not exist yet. So this entry ratifies what the
    14	glossary already says rather than changing anything, which is the Q-0085 shape.
    15	
    16	**The measurement that decides the second half.** A deferral is not the rare case anyone assumed. A
    17	range is deferred whenever an earlier group of the flow creates one of its endpoints, and
    18	`packages/core/src/engine/diff.ts` classifies it that way **even when the ref already exists at run
    19	start** — its own words, because *"bytes captured before its producer ran are that step's PREVIOUS
    20	output."* `harness/flows/chore.yaml:32` diffs
    21	`harness/{id}/integration...harness/{id}/implement` while `implement` at `:6` is the step that
    22	creates the right endpoint, and that line has read so since the flow was created (Q-0008, `3b8eb1e`,
    23	the only commit to touch it). **So the chore flow's review range is deferred on every run the engine
    24	was capable of deferring on — 45 of the 50 `flow=chore start` lines this backlog records.** The other
    25	five are 2026-08-24's, which predate the deferred-range handling Q-0035 landed on 2026-08-25
    26	(`dad6254`). Not one of the 45 was wrong to say nothing.
    27	
    28	An `info` per deferred range would therefore print, on every run, that the ordinary thing is about to
    29	happen. That is the reassurance *"The board reports push lag, and never a CI conclusion"*
    30	(2026-09-06) refused in its own domain, and the asymmetry it named holds here for the same reason:
    31	**the instrument may warn and may never reassure.** This entry extends that from a board fact to a
    32	preflight one.
    33	
    34	**What is emitted is what is actionable, at the moment it is actionable**, and `diff.ts` already
    35	distinguishes the two ways a deferred range can go wrong, and within the first of them it
    36	distinguishes **which** step owed **which** ref. Where an **endpoint does not resolve**, the
    37	missing-endpoint diagnosis branches on whether a producer owns the ref that failed: the failing
    38	endpoint's own producer is named as ``step "${producer.step}" was expected to create
    39	${producer.ref}``, while a producer of the *other* endpoint explains the deferral without being made
    40	to owe the failure — ``the range was deferred waiting for step "${producer.step}" to create
    41	${producer.ref}``. The code's own comment states the rule: such a producer *"is never phrased as
    42	owing the ref that failed, because no step owed that one"* (Q-0038). And where
    43	the range **resolves but spans nothing**, `emptyRangeFailure` takes a deferral-aware remedy instead:
    44	``check that step "${deferred.step}" committed its work to ${deferred.ref}``, in place of the
    45	*"review it before it becomes contained"* advice a pre-existing range gets. Its own comment gives the
    46	reason — for a range this run deferred, the endpoint *"never became contained — it started that way,
    47	because that step committed nothing."* So the deferral is not merely remembered; it changes what the
    48	reader is told, in both failure paths, at the moment either can be acted on. Silence at run start
    49	costs nothing because nothing is withheld.
    50	
    51	**Alternatives considered.** *Emit one `info` per deferred range at run start, naming the range and
    52	its producing step* — `deferredDiffs` holds both, so it is cheap; rejected on the measurement above,
    53	because it is a line on every run for the commonest outcome, and because the same information
    54	already reaches the reader in the only case where it changes anything. *Amend the skipped-subject
    55	rule to say a deferral is not a skip* — rejected as unnecessary rather than wrong: the rule is about
    56	declining to examine, the glossary already scopes the preflight's guarantee per endpoint, and
    57	amending a landed entry to say something it never claimed would weaken a rule that is doing its job
    58	elsewhere.
    59	
    60	**Why.** Q-0051's OQ-1 and Q-0052's ticket body both framed this as *whether the `--dry` placeholder
    61	discharges the reporting rule*, and Q-0052's R-6 measured that the placeholder **reaches nobody**:
    62	`runAgentStep` returns at the dry short-circuit above `allocateOccurrence` and `persistArtifact`, so
    63	it is never persisted, emitted or shown, and under a real run `materialiseDiff` means it is never
    64	produced. A string in a discarded buffer is not a report and this entry does not treat it as one.
    65	What that leaves is a narrower question than the two the ticket posed, and the narrower question has
    66	a measured answer.
    67	
    68	**Not decided here.** Whether `--dry` should name the ranges it would defer. `--dry`'s output is a
    69	reporting surface whose whole purpose is to say what each step would do, and today it says
    70	`dry run — prompt N chars` (`packages/core/src/engine/steps.ts:234`), a number in which a deferral is
    71	invisible. That is a change to what a command prints, with its own subject and its own test, and it
    72	is a ticket rather than a clause of this one. It is stated here so it is a live question rather than
    73	an obligation buried in a closed ticket's entry.
     1	name: chore
     2	consumes: requirements
     3	produces: reviewed
     4	cross_vendor: required
     5	steps:
     6	  - id: implement
     7	    role: developer-generalist
     8	    adapter: claude
     9	    worktree: true
    10	    branch: "harness/{id}/implement"
    11	    base: "harness/{id}/integration"
    12	    input:
    13	      backlog: [requirements/merged.md, requirements/errata.md, "review/chore/run-{run}/chore-iter-*.md"]
    14	      harness: [rules.md, architecture.md]
    15	      repo: true
    16	    output: { writes: ["dev/chore/run-{run}/implement-iter-{iter}.md"] }
    17	    instructions: >
    18	      Implement exactly what requirements/merged.md asks for and nothing else: no scope
    19	      creep, no speculative abstraction, no tidying of code you were not sent to change.
    20	      If a review report from an earlier iteration is present, this is a revision round —
    21	      address every finding explicitly. If the requirement is ambiguous or contradictory,
    22	      stop and say so in the summary rather than improvising a decision it does not
    23	      authorise. Commit nothing; the harness commits your worktree. Write a report of what
    24	      you changed file by file and what you deliberately left alone — the reviewer reads
    25	      it beside your diff.
    26	
    27	  - id: review
    28	    role: code-reviewer
    29	    adapter: codex
    30	    input:
    31	      backlog: [requirements/merged.md, requirements/errata.md, "dev/chore/run-{run}/implement-iter-*.md"]
    32	      diff: "harness/{id}/integration...harness/{id}/implement"
    33	    output:
    34	      writes: ["review/chore/run-{run}/chore-iter-{iter}.md"]
    35	      verdict: approve|revise
    36	    instructions: >
    37	      Review the diff against the requirement and the implementer's report. Judge whether
    38	      the change does what the requirement asks and only that: unrequested scope, a default
    39	      nobody chose, and configuration that contradicts the repository's rules are findings.
    40	      This is read-only — do not edit any file. Write every finding as
    41	      "blocker: <file>:<line> <what is wrong and what to do>", with the prefix exactly
    42	      blocker, major or nit and the file:line reference always present; output in any other
    43	      shape is rejected and the run stops. Approve only when no blocker or major survives;
    44	      nits alone approve, and a nit you have is reported rather than dropped. On approve every
    45	      finding must be a nit; on revise there must be at least one finding.
    46	    on_fail: { goto: implement, max_iterations: 2, on_exhausted: gate }
    47	
    48	  # No on_fail: a conflict with the base branch, or a suite red for a reason this flow did
    49	  # not introduce, is not work the implementer can do — its worktree branches from the
    50	  # ticket branch, where nothing is wrong. Stop with the reason rather than spend a budget.
    51	  - id: integrate
    52	    type: integrate
    53	    branches: ["harness/{id}/implement"]
    54	    into: "harness/{id}/integration"
    55	    run_tests: true
    56	    expect: pass
    57	    output: { writes: ["dev/chore/run-{run}/integration.md"] }
    58	
    59	  - gate: human
    60	    reason: Chore owner approves the reviewed change now on the ticket branch, with the suite green

docs/decisions/040-erratum-m1s-closing-entry-on-q-0006s-empty-diff.md:6:**Why it is worth correcting rather than leaving.** `materialiseDiff` hard-codes this diagnosis: an empty range throws *"is empty because `<branch>` is already merged into `<base>`, so there is nothing left to review"* whenever `merge-base --is-ancestor` succeeds, and the generic message otherwise. That is good error design and it inherited a conclusion nobody re-derived. If the cause here was something else — a rollback by `finish()`, a branch reset, a range built from the wrong ref — then the engine will keep explaining a class of failure with the one story that was written down. backlog/Q-0036-what-green-means-and-where-the-code-is/requirements/merged.md:73:No state is ever inferred from a ticket's stage, its branch name, its `history`, identical file trees, the working tree, or a remote — two refs with the same tree and no ancestry relationship are *not contained*. The board performs no `fetch`, `pull`, `ls-remote` or any other subcommand that contacts a remote, does not deepen a shallow clone, and behaves identically offline. Every git invocation passes an argv array to `execFileSync`, never a shell string, and treats `t.meta.branch` as untrusted input disambiguated as `refs/heads/<branch>`: a ticket whose `branch` is `--upload-pack=touch pwned` renders unannotated or indeterminate, creates no file named `pwned`, and exits 0. The containment helper lives in `spike/src/git.js` beside the existing argv rule, not inline in `spike/bin/harness.js`, so `materialiseDiff`'s duplicate closure has one place to move in M2's port.
backlog/Q-0036-what-green-means-and-where-the-code-is/requirements/merged.md:107:`docs/DECISIONS.md` gains an append-only entry in the required shape — title with date, **Decision**, **Alternatives considered**, **Why** — recording that containment is derived from git on each board invocation and never stored. Its alternatives are a `landed:` field in `ticket.md` frontmatter, a stage after `deployed`, and computing it once into a cache under `.quorum/`; each is rejected with its reason (a copy of a git fact in mutable state drifts, and a wrong field is believed; a stage would make merging a flow-advanced transition when merging is a human act outside every flow, and `deployed` belongs to Q-0012; a cache is the same drift with an extra file). The entry also records AC-2's exit-code mapping and AC-2's shallow asymmetry — a positive survives truncated history, a negative does not — as the product's rule for reading git ancestry, so nobody re-derives or "simplifies" it later. `docs/GLOSSARY.md` defines **Containment** — the git-derived relationship between a ticket branch tip and the configured base branch, with its three states — before the term's second use, introducing no synonym for it (not "merged", "landed", "shipped" or "in main") and no synonym for **stage**, **ticket**, **flow** or **base branch**. `docs/06-development-plan.md` lists Q-0034, Q-0035 and Q-0036 as M2 work with one line each, without rewriting the historical M1 record or its carried-forward items, and its status line is bumped.

backlog/Q-0050-core-engine-run-loop/review/round-3/claude.md:32:Q-0051's `ctx.diffInputs` and `ctx.deferredDiffs` are safe by accident — they are `Map`s created at context construction, so they are on `context` before the spread. backlog/Q-0050-core-engine-run-loop/review/round-3/claude.md:161:`engine.js:740` is `String(s).replace(…)`; the port takes `template: string` and calls `.replace` directly. The coercion is not decoration — every call site interpolates a value that came out of YAML (`step.run`, `step.branch`, `s.into`, `site.input.diff`), and YAML will hand back a number for `branch: 2` or `run: 2024`. Under the port that becomes `TypeError: template.replace is not a function` at whichever of Q-0051's or Q-0052's call sites reaches it first, in place of the substitution the spike performed. Charter §2 preserves behaviour; restore `String(template)`, or state in the JSDoc that coercion is now each caller's, so Q-0052 writes `String(step.run)` deliberately rather than discovering it.

backlog/Q-0051-core-engine-diff-preflight/requirements/archive/run-1-aborted/candidate-codex.md:1:# Q-0051 — core/engine diff preflight and materialisation
backlog/Q-0051-core-engine-diff-preflight/requirements/archive/run-1-aborted/candidate-codex.md:39:6. **String conversion at interpolation boundaries.** Because `interpolate` accepts only a string, the port deliberately applies `String(...)` before interpolating each runtime value read from the flow shape: `site.input.diff`, an explicit `branch`, and `into`. `materialiseDiff` likewise preserves its existing `String(step.input.diff)` conversion. A YAML numeric value such as `branch: 2` compiles and is interpreted as the string `"2"`; this conversion does not otherwise change interpolation semantics.
backlog/Q-0051-core-engine-diff-preflight/requirements/archive/run-1-aborted/candidate-codex.md:50:9. **Range guard.** `materialiseDiff` requires exactly two non-empty endpoints joined by exactly `...`. Each endpoint must be either:
backlog/Q-0051-core-engine-diff-preflight/requirements/archive/run-1-aborted/candidate-codex.md:56:10. **Diff anchor behavior.** `materialiseDiff` derives the allowed base and `{base}` anchor from `ctx.vars.base`, falling back only when that value is absent to `config.repo.base_branch`, then `main`, as in the spike. A caller-supplied `RunFlowOptions.base` therefore changes the diff anchor and range-guard base. It does not change `config.repo.base_branch` or any merge-source selection.

backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:1:# Q-0052 — core/engine agent, gate and script steps
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:5:`packages/core` can route a flow, but it cannot execute agent or script steps, and its declared gate-step behavior is incomplete. Those step kinds still reject as owned by Q-0052. As a result, the CLI cannot yet run the `qa-red`, `qa-final`, deploy, or chore flows through `core`.
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:23:1. **Agent-step execution replaces the Q-0052 placeholder.** On the `packages/core` surface, a non-gate step that is neither `script`, `integrate`, nor `fan_out` executes as an agent step. The implementation ports `runAgentStep` and its required `buildPrompt`, `schemaFor`, `resolveModel`, `reviewRound`, and `formatCost` behavior. `integrate` and `fan_out` remain unavailable with ownership attributed to Q-0053.
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:27:3. **Input interpolation deliberately accepts YAML scalar values.** Every Q-0052 call site converts a possibly non-string YAML value with `String(...)` before calling the typed `interpolate(string, vars)` function. This applies at minimum to `step.run`, `step.branch`, every declarative write destination such as `s.into`, and every `site.input.diff`. A fixture with a numeric value such as `branch: 2` proves that the value is interpolated as text and does not cause a TypeScript escape hatch or implicit coercion inside `interpolate`.
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:31:5. **The known preflight cache defect is preserved and cited.** When `ctx.diffInputs` already contains an entry for a range, `buildPrompt` continues to prefer that cached entry even if the site was classified as deferred. The read site contains one short authority comment in the form `Why: preserved defect, see Q-0038 E-3(b) / Q-0078.` A focused regression test pins this current cached-byte behavior. The discriminating red scenario and the eventual behavior change belong to Q-0078.
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:39:9. **Invalid structured output remains an explicit, inspectable failure.** When extracted output violates the generated schema, the unvalidated raw agent text is saved beside the ticket using the established core persistence contract, and the run stops with a message that identifies invalid structured output and where the raw text was saved. Wrapper tolerance remains exclusively in Q-0046's `extractJson`; Q-0052 does not broaden extraction or merge extraction with schema validation.
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:43:11. **Agent occurrences are allocated and registered atomically.** The run-history seam exposes an operation equivalent to `allocateOccurrence(step, kind, fields)` that both allocates an `Occurrence` and registers it as active before adapter or command execution can fail. No caller can allocate an unregistered active occurrence. Agent occurrences retain their exact `prompt.txt` and `output.txt` artifacts and are finalized through the existing run-history contract.
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:61:20. **The port removes the gate test's artificial timer.** `askGate` and its new Vitest fixtures do not create the spike's one-second `signalWindow` timer. The waiting fixture owns a pending promise with its own libuv handle or uses an equivalent deterministic Vitest mechanism. Removing this timer is the sole authorized test-driven behavior cleanup in Q-0052; other spike behavior remains unchanged.
backlog/Q-0052-core-engine-agent-gate-script-steps/requirements/candidate-codex.md:75:27. **No step execution writes to the user's working tree.** Tests prove that code-writing agent operations and project commands execute only in the repository/worktree context supplied by core, with execution worktrees under `.harness/worktrees/`. Persistent run data remains under the ticket folder or `.quorum/` according to the existing contracts. Q-0052 does not weaken the worktree guards supplied by earlier tickets.

backlog/Q-0050-core-engine-run-loop/review/round-6/codex.md:5:- **major** — `packages/core/src/engine/engine.ts:143`: Step-ID enrichment relies on one mutable run-wide `stepId`, set from the outer step at `engine.ts:225`, but `routing.ts:58` executes members of a `parallel` group concurrently. All adapter events from those members therefore receive the outer group’s ID—often `"undefined"`—rather than the executing member’s ID. A single mutable slot also cannot represent multiple concurrently active members. This violates AC-2’s requirement that each adapter event gain its executing step ID and will cause M3 consumers to attribute parallel output to the wrong task. Preserve the shared mutable run context while giving each parallel member a member-scoped emitter, or enrich adapter callbacks inside `runAgentStep` from that member’s ID; add a parallel test asserting distinct member IDs.

backlog/Q-0050-core-engine-run-loop/requirements/merged.md:321:**Codex's document was struck back hard on size and on one substantive error.** Nineteen criteria carrying roughly a hundred sub-clauses is four to six times the ticket a downstream flow can carry, and much of it restates the same obligation in three places. backlog/Q-0011-run-history-on-disk/review/round-1/verdict.md:22:**blocker 1 — `spike/src/engine.js:324` · the internal `_started` field is persisted, so mid-run manifests violate their own frozen schema.** `allocateOccurrence` stamps `_started` on the object pushed into `ctx.history.manifest.steps`, removed only at `:334`; `replaceManifest` (`:352`) serialises the whole snapshot. backlog/Q-0011-run-history-on-disk/solution/review-round-4.md:17:**Occurrence allocation is deterministic by construction.** `run-history-writer.contract.md:36-40` requires synchronous allocation at step-attempt start, ordered by allocation rather than completion. That is not a stylistic preference: `runStep` fans parallel siblings out with `Promise.allSettled(step.parallel.map(...))` (`engine.js:120`), and `runAgentStep` runs `loadRole`, `getAdapter`, `ensureWorktree` and `buildPrompt` synchronously before its first `await`. Allocating there — and only there — makes the parallel directory names reproducible, which is what AC-4's test needs. Getting this right is the difference between a testable contract and a flaky one.
backlog/Q-0011-run-history-on-disk/solution/review-round-4.md:79:`run-manifest.schema.json:58` and `run-events.schema.json:294-301` fix `kind` at `adapter | script | integrate | gate`. `engine.js:137` dispatches a fifth case: `if (step.fan_out) return runFanOut(step, ctx);`. `runFanOut` (`engine.js:429-441`) is a real step with its own `step.id`, its own `ui.info` lines under that id, and a wave loop that calls `runAgentStep` per task.

   276	  if (!left || !right || extra.length || !related(left) || !related(right)) {
   277	    throw new FlowError(`${String(step.id)}: input.diff must relate the configured base or this ticket's own branches ("${base}", "${ticketPrefix}…") with "...", got ${range}`);
   278	  }
   279	  // Which endpoint an earlier step of THIS flow was supposed to create, when the preflight deferred
   280	  // this range. Naming that step is the difference between telling the reader a branch is missing and
   281	  // telling them the producing step committed nothing.
   282	  const deferred = context.deferredDiffs.get(range) ?? null;
   283	  // One spawn per endpoint answers both "does it resolve?" and "to what?" — and the SHA is what makes
   284	  // the failure re-checkable tomorrow, after the branch tips have moved.
   285	  const sha = { left: shortSha(context.repoDir, left), right: shortSha(context.repoDir, right) };
   286	  for (const side of ['left', 'right'] as const) {

   288	    if (sha[side] != null) continue;
   289	    const other: EndpointSide = side === 'left' ? 'right' : 'left';
   290	    const otherRef = other === 'left' ? left : right;
   291	    const otherSha = sha[other];
   292	    throw missingEndpointFailure(step, context, {
   293	      side, ref, range, written, base,
   294	      clauses: [
   295	        otherSha != null
   296	          ? `the ${other} endpoint ${otherRef} resolves to ${otherSha}`
   297	          : `the ${other} endpoint ${otherRef} does not resolve either`,
   298	        // Which step owed which ref, whichever endpoint went bad. The failing endpoint's own producer
   299	        // is named as the step that was expected to create it; a producer of the OTHER endpoint
   300	        // explains why the range was deferred and is never phrased as owing the ref that failed,
   301	        // because no step owed that one. Both are kept when both endpoints were deferred, so a
   302	        // reversal of endpoint order cannot hide either. See Q-0038.
   303	        ...(deferred?.producers ?? []).map((producer) => producer.ref === ref

   324	/** Everything {@link emptyRangeFailure} quotes, gathered by the caller that already read it. */
   325	interface EmptyRange {
   326	  step: DiffStep;
   327	  written: string;
   328	  range: string;
   329	  left: string;
   330	  right: string;
   331	  sha: { left: string | null; right: string | null };
   332	  deferred: DeferredDiff | null;
   333	  context: DiffContext;
   334	}
   335	
   336	/**
   337	 * The message an empty range stops the run with: evidence, and no claim about how it came about.
   338	 *
   339	 * An empty range is never a reviewable state and it must not be one silently — a review once paid
   340	 * two vendors to read zero bytes and returned a verdict the engine acted on. That the run stops is
   341	 * settled; what it stops WITH is Q-0035's subject.
   342	 *
   343	 * The message this replaced reported a historical event from an ancestry check, which establishes a
   344	 * relationship between two commits and says nothing about the route by which it arose. It named no
   345	 * SHA, so it could not be re-checked once the branch tips moved, which is exactly when someone wants
   346	 * to. And it recommended a range the guard above refuses.
   347	 *
   348	 * So: the range as written and as interpolated, both endpoints with the short SHA each resolved to,
   349	 * the check verbatim, and its outcome — then nothing git did not return. Each outcome is tied to an
   350	 * exit code and to nothing else; every further branch would be a new claim that can be wrong in the
   351	 * way this function exists to stop being wrong. The vocabulary is the board's, recorded under
   352	 * Containment in the glossary: "contained", and never a word asserting an event. The spaced form in
   353	 * the "adds nothing since its merge base" line survives because it names the commit a three-dot range
   354	 * is defined against; the command git spells with a hyphen is quoted by `git/git.ts`, which is the
   355	 * one file that runs it and the one file a source guard lets carry its name.
   356	 */
   357	function emptyRangeFailure({ step, written, range, left, right, sha, deferred, context }: EmptyRange): string {
   358	  const { check, sameTree } = emptyRangeEvidence(context.repoDir, left, right);
   359	  const outcome = check.state === 'contained' ? 'contained'
   360	    : check.state === 'not-contained' ? 'not contained'
   361	      : `indeterminate (${check.reason}${check.detail ? `: ${check.detail}` : ''})`;
   362	  const committed = deferred
   363	    ? `check that step "${deferred.step}" committed its work to ${deferred.ref}`
   364	    : `check that the ticket's work was committed to ${right}`;
   365	  const [diagnosis, remedy] = check.state === 'contained'
   366	    ? [`${right} is contained in ${left}, so the range spans no commits. That is a relationship between the two commits above, not a record of how it came about.`,
   367	      // "Review it before it becomes contained" is the right next move only when the endpoint
   368	      // pre-dates the run. For a range this run deferred, the endpoint was created moments ago by a
   369	      // step of this very flow, so it never *became* contained — it started that way, because that
   370	      // step committed nothing. Sending the reader to review earlier is advice about a state that
   371	      // never arose.
   372	      deferred ? committed : `review ${right} before it becomes contained in ${left}`]
   373	    : check.state === 'indeterminate'
   374	      ? [`git could not answer whether ${right} is contained in ${left}, so this failure reports the emptiness and claims nothing further.`,
   375	        're-run the check above and fix whatever stopped git answering']
   376	      : [`${right} is not contained in ${left}, and the range is still empty.`
   377	        + (sameTree === true ? ` ${left} and ${right} are different commits holding identical trees.`
   378	          : sameTree === false ? ` ${right} adds nothing since its merge base with ${left}.`
   379	            : ''),
   380	        committed];
   381	  return [
   382	    `${String(step.id)}: ${named(range, written)} is empty — git diff --stat printed nothing.`,
   383	    `  left endpoint   ${left} = ${sha.left}`,
   384	    `  right endpoint  ${right} = ${sha.right}`,
   385	    deferred ? `  produced by     step "${deferred.step}", which was expected to create ${deferred.ref}` : null,
   386	    `  containment     \`${check.command}\` → ${outcome}`,
   387	    `  ${diagnosis}`,
   388	    `  Remedy: ${remedy}.`,
   389	  ].filter((line): line is string => line !== null).join('\n');
   390	}
   391	
   392	/**
   393	 * `bytes` cut back to the last complete UTF-8 character, so a truncated patch never ends mid-code
   394	 * point. A buffer of continuation bytes alone, and an empty one, are returned unchanged: neither
   395	 * holds a boundary this can find, and the scan stops at the final candidate lead byte either way.
   396	 */
   397	export function trimIncompleteUtf8Suffix(bytes: Buffer): Buffer {
   398	  if (!bytes.length) return bytes;
   399	  let lead = bytes.length - 1;
   400	  while (lead >= 0 && (bytes[lead]! & 0xc0) === 0x80) lead -= 1;
   401	  if (lead < 0) return bytes;
   402	  const first = bytes[lead]!;
   403	  const width = first < 0x80 ? 1 : first >= 0xc2 && first <= 0xdf ? 2 : first >= 0xe0 && first <= 0xef ? 3 : first >= 0xf0 && first <= 0xf4 ? 4 : 1;
   404	  return bytes.length - lead < width ? bytes.subarray(0, lead) : bytes;
   405	}
   406	
   407	/**
   408	 * Judges every diff site in `context.flow`, in flow order, before any step runs.
   409	 *
   410	 * A range whose endpoints all already exist is materialised once into `context.diffInputs`; a range
   411	 * holding an endpoint an earlier group creates is recorded in `context.deferredDiffs`, and every
   412	 * endpoint beside it is still proven now. The first range that cannot be satisfied stops the run,
   413	 * even when another distinct range was valid. Identical under `--dry` and under a real run: there is
   414	 * no branch on `context.dry` here and none belongs here.
   415	 *
   416	 * @throws {FlowError} for an out-of-class range, an endpoint that does not resolve, or an empty one.
   417	 * @throws {TypeError} for a flow carrying no `steps`, which is read directly and uncoalesced.
   418	 */
   419	export function preflightDiffs(context: PreflightContext): void {
   420	  const { flow } = context;
   421	  // ref → the id of the earliest step that creates it. A Set answered "is this deferred?"; the map
   422	  // also answers "deferred waiting on whom?", which is what lets a deferred range that turns out
   423	  // empty at step time tell the reader the producing step committed nothing rather than that a branch
   424	  // is missing.
   425	  const createdSoFar = new Map<string, string>();
   426	  const remember = (ref: string, stepId: string): void => {
   427	    if (!createdSoFar.has(ref)) createdSoFar.set(ref, stepId);
   428	  };
   429	  // `flow.steps` is iterated directly rather than through a local binding, so a flow with no `steps`
   430	  // key throws here naming the expression the reader can find — the same first line the terminal
   431	  // note, the runs.log line and the terminal event then carry.
   432	  for (const group of flow.steps as unknown as ReadonlyArray<Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>>) {
   433	    const members = (group.parallel ?? [group]) as ReadonlyArray<Readonly<Record<string, unknown>>>;
   434	    // Judge every diff in the group against branches created strictly before the group: a parallel
   435	    // sibling's branch is concurrent, not earlier.
   436	    for (const { site, perTask } of members.flatMap(diffSitesOf)) {
   437	      const written = String(site.input.diff);
   438	      const range = interpolate(written, context.vars);
   439	      const endpoints = classifyEndpoints(range, createdSoFar, perTask);
   440	      if (endpoints.every((endpoint) => endpoint.class === 'pre-existing')) {
   441	        // Why: preserved defect, see Q-0078 — the cache is keyed by the interpolated range alone, so
   442	        // a site materialising `X...Y` before a later group creates `Y` leaves bytes a correctly
   443	        // deferred second site then reads back. Unreachable in every shipped flow, and its obvious
   444	        // fix collides with the identical-bytes guarantee the once-per-range rule exists for.
   445	        if (!context.diffInputs.has(range)) context.diffInputs.set(range, materialiseDiff(site, context));
   446	        continue;
   447	      }
   448	      const producers = endpoints.filter((endpoint): endpoint is EndpointProducer => endpoint.class === 'step-created');
   449	      // A half-interpolated key can never be looked up at step time, so recording one would be a
   450	      // record nothing reads.
   451	      if (producers.length > 0 && !endpoints.some((endpoint) => endpoint.class === 'template')) {
   452	        context.deferredDiffs.set(range, { ref: producers[0].ref, step: producers[0].step, producers });
   453	      }
   454	      // Every endpoint that is due is proven now, where it costs nothing — one endpoint being owed by
   455	      // a later step says nothing about the other. See Q-0038.
   456	      for (const endpoint of endpoints) {
   457	        if (endpoint.class !== 'pre-existing' || shortSha(context.repoDir, endpoint.ref) != null) continue;
   458	        throw missingEndpointFailure(site, context, {
   459	          side: endpoint.side, ref: endpoint.ref, range, written, base: String(context.vars.base),
   460	          clauses: [notDueClause(endpoints.find((other) => other !== endpoint), site)],
   461	        });
   462	      }
   463	    }
   464	    for (const s of members) {
   465	      // Two renderings of the same absent id, both preserved. A step with no `id` — which lint does
   466	      // not yet refuse; see Q-0055 — names its branch `harness/<ticket>/undefined`, as the worktree
   467	      // step itself does, while the producer a diagnostic then quotes reads `null`, which is what the
   468	      // spike's own `?? null` puts there. Collapsing them would change one message or the other.
   469	      const stepId = String(s.id ?? null);
   470	      const defaultBranch = ticketBranch(context.ticket.meta.id, String(s.id));
   471	      if (s.worktree) remember(interpolate(String(s.branch ?? defaultBranch), context.vars), stepId);
   472	      if (s.type === 'integrate' && s.into) remember(interpolate(String(s.into), context.vars), stepId);
   473	    }
   474	  }
   475	}
76-    config: { repo: { base_branch: 'main' } },
77-    vars,
78-    ticket: ticketRecord(repoDir),
79-    runId: 1,
80-    baseOverride: null,
81:    deferredDiffs: new Map<string, DeferredDiff>(),
82-    persistence: { appendLog: () => { /* no sink in a direct call */ } },
83-    ...overrides,
84-  };

     1	# A deferral is not a skip, and the preflight says nothing about one — 2026-09-07
     2	
     3	**Decision:** the run-level diff preflight goes on emitting **nothing** when it defers a range, and
     4	*"Q-0035 accepted: a check that skips its subject must not report success"* (2026-08-25) is **not
     5	amended**, because it does not reach this case. Q-0082 offered those two as its alternatives; the
     6	answer is neither, and no code changes.
     7	
     8	**A deferral is not a decline to examine.** The rule governs a check that could have examined its
     9	subject and did not. `docs/GLOSSARY.md`'s **Preflight** entry already states the preflight's
    10	guarantee as *per endpoint, not per range* — every endpoint that is **due** is resolved at run start,
    11	and it *"never resolves an endpoint that is not due, and never reports one as having failed to
    12	resolve."* An endpoint an earlier step of the same flow has yet to create is not a subject the
    13	preflight passed over; it is a subject that does not exist yet. So this entry ratifies what the
    14	glossary already says rather than changing anything, which is the Q-0085 shape.
    15	
    16	**The measurement that decides the second half.** A deferral is not the rare case anyone assumed. A
    17	range is deferred whenever an earlier group of the flow creates one of its endpoints, and
    18	`packages/core/src/engine/diff.ts` classifies it that way **even when the ref already exists at run
    19	start** — its own words, because *"bytes captured before its producer ran are that step's PREVIOUS
    20	output."* `harness/flows/chore.yaml:32` diffs
    21	`harness/{id}/integration...harness/{id}/implement` while `implement` at `:6` is the step that
    22	creates the right endpoint, and that line has read so since the flow was created (Q-0008, `3b8eb1e`,
    23	the only commit to touch it). **So the chore flow's review range is deferred on every run the engine
    24	was capable of deferring on — 45 of the 50 `flow=chore start` lines this backlog records.** The other
    25	five are 2026-08-24's, which predate the deferred-range handling Q-0035 landed on 2026-08-25
    26	(`dad6254`). Not one of the 45 was wrong to say nothing.
    27	
    28	An `info` per deferred range would therefore print, on every run, that the ordinary thing is about to
    29	happen. That is the reassurance *"The board reports push lag, and never a CI conclusion"*
    30	(2026-09-06) refused in its own domain, and the asymmetry it named holds here for the same reason:
    31	**the instrument may warn and may never reassure.** This entry extends that from a board fact to a
    32	preflight one.
    33	
    34	**What is emitted is what is actionable, at the moment it is actionable**, and `diff.ts` already
    35	distinguishes the two ways a deferred range can go wrong, and within the first of them it
    36	distinguishes **which** step owed **which** ref. Where an **endpoint does not resolve**, the
    37	missing-endpoint diagnosis branches on whether a producer owns the ref that failed: the failing
    38	endpoint's own producer is named as ``step "${producer.step}" was expected to create
    39	${producer.ref}``, while a producer of the *other* endpoint explains the deferral without being made
    40	to owe the failure — ``the range was deferred waiting for step "${producer.step}" to create
    41	${producer.ref}``. The code's own comment states the rule: such a producer *"is never phrased as
    42	owing the ref that failed, because no step owed that one"* (Q-0038). And where
    43	the range **resolves but spans nothing**, `emptyRangeFailure` takes a deferral-aware remedy instead:
    44	``check that step "${deferred.step}" committed its work to ${deferred.ref}``, in place of the
    45	*"review it before it becomes contained"* advice a pre-existing range gets. Its own comment gives the
    46	reason — for a range this run deferred, the endpoint *"never became contained — it started that way,
    47	because that step committed nothing."* So the deferral is not merely remembered; it changes what the
    48	reader is told, in both failure paths, at the moment either can be acted on. Silence at run start
    49	costs nothing because nothing is withheld.
    50	
    51	**Alternatives considered.** *Emit one `info` per deferred range at run start, naming the range and
    52	its producing step* — `deferredDiffs` holds both, so it is cheap; rejected on the measurement above,
    53	because it is a line on every run for the commonest outcome, and because the same information
    54	already reaches the reader in the only case where it changes anything. *Amend the skipped-subject
    55	rule to say a deferral is not a skip* — rejected as unnecessary rather than wrong: the rule is about
    56	declining to examine, the glossary already scopes the preflight's guarantee per endpoint, and
    57	amending a landed entry to say something it never claimed would weaken a rule that is doing its job
    58	elsewhere.
    59	
    60	**Why.** Q-0051's OQ-1 and Q-0052's ticket body both framed this as *whether the `--dry` placeholder
    61	discharges the reporting rule*, and Q-0052's R-6 measured that the placeholder **reaches nobody**:
    62	`runAgentStep` returns at the dry short-circuit above `allocateOccurrence` and `persistArtifact`, so
    63	it is never persisted, emitted or shown, and under a real run `materialiseDiff` means it is never
    64	produced. A string in a discarded buffer is not a report and this entry does not treat it as one.
    65	What that leaves is a narrower question than the two the ticket posed, and the narrower question has
    66	a measured answer.
    67	
    68	**Not decided here.** Whether `--dry` should name the ranges it would defer. `--dry`'s output is a
    69	reporting surface whose whole purpose is to say what each step would do, and today it says
    70	`dry run — prompt N chars` (`packages/core/src/engine/steps.ts:234`), a number in which a deferral is
    71	invisible. That is a change to what a command prints, with its own subject and its own test, and it
    72	is a ticket rather than a clause of this one. It is stated here so it is a live question rather than
    73	an obligation buried in a closed ticket's entry.

nit `docs/decisions/081-a-deferral-is-not-a-skip.md:43` — The entry says `emptyRangeFailure` takes a deferral-aware remedy whenever a deferred range resolves but is empty.
